# On Free Will: Greek Text (De autexusio : texte grec)

**Author**: Méthode d'Olympe  
**Series**: PO Tome 22  
**Columns**: pp. 726–836  

[p. 726] {#po.22.p726}

### METHOD
ΚΑΤΑΓῸ Μὲ Δ ΕΙΛ'
[OF FREE WILL]

### METHOD
«ἐῆππλ ϑιηληπηήοκατοῦ ὦ πλ ΗΝ ΠΚα ὦ πο5Ὲ ἠθ ὦ πεμῃ ἡ ὦ οΛΙΙΟΛΛΟΤΕΟΤΕΈ. Γ ᾿ τοέπσαν πλαγθελοκη ὠτκνε. ΜΕΘΟΔΙΟΥ͂ ΠΕΡῚ ΤΟΥ ΑΥ̓ΤΕΞΟΥΣΙΟΥ ΚΑΤΑΓῸ ΜΕ Δ ΕΙΛ' «ἔπππλ ϑιηληπηήοκλτοῦ ἡ ΠΔΎΘΝΗΚΑ ὦ πο5Ὲ ἀρ ὦ πεμῃ ἡ ὦ οΛΙΙΟΕΛΛΟΤΕΟΤΕΈ. β ᾿ ΜΕΘΟΔΙΟΥ͂

The old man of Ithaca, according to the pagan fable, to whom his unbridled passion for song made him desire to hear the Sirens, sailed captive toward Sicily and stopped the ears of his companions; not that he envied them the hearing of this song, not that he himself loved to cover himself with chains, but

Ηό Α3ὰ ΤλκΟΓῸ Τλλολ Ἠὲ ἘΜΒΑΝ ποολογχαταΐ, ΝῊ χόμογ ΟΉΡΜΗΣ πορλλιιάτη πατφόκηλα " ποιμηχα ἢ ΕΥΤΤΕ ἦχπε ΜΟΛΎΛΗΪΕ ΠΟΤΡΕΝΈΕ "ἢ ἐοτε, “λκύμχ᾽ Βοπεοτβέηλτο πὲ ΠπλῚς ἩΈΒΟΟΓΟ ΜΟΛῸ ΟΑ ΜΛΟΛΑΖΚΑΤῊ ΤλλΟλ, ὄτοπε πηόσοιλε Ὁ μὲν Ἰθαχήσιος γέρων, κατὰ τὸν τῶν Ἑλλήνων μῦθον, τῆς Σειρήνων βουλόμενος
because the end of the song of the Sirens was death for those who listened to it. That is what the singing Sirens of the pagans were. For me, it is not to such a song that I make myself the listener, and I do not wish to hear the Sirens singing over the tombs of men, whose silence is better for men than their voice. It is to the hearing of a divine voice that I vow to take pleasure, a voice that I can hear often without ceasing to
ἀχοῦσαι δῆς διὰ τὴν τῆς φωνῆς ἀχύλαστον ἡδονήν, δεσμώτης ἔπλει εἰς τὴν Σιχελίαν καὶ τὰς τῶν ἑταίρων ἐνέφραττεν ἀκοάς, οὐχ ἐκείνοις ἕνεκα τῆς ἀκροάσεως φθονῶν, οὐδὲ ἑαυτὸν δεσμῷ περιόάλλειν ἐπιθυμῶν, ἀλλ᾽ ὅτι τέλος τῆς ἐχείνων ᾧδῆς τοῖς ἀκούουσι θάνατος ἦν᾽ τοιαῦται γὰρ" παρ᾽ Ἕλλησιν ἦδον αἱ Σειρῆνες᾽. ᾿Εγὼ δὲ τοιαύτης μὲν φδῆς ἀχροατὴς οὐ γίνομαι, οὐδὲ ἀκούειν ἐπιθυμῶ Σειρήνων ἐπιτάφιον ἀνθρώπων ἀδουσῶν, ὧν ἡ σιγὴ τῆς φωνῆς" τοῖς ἀνθρώποις χρησιμωτέρα γίνεται" θείας δέ" τινος ἀπολαύειν φωνῆς εὔχομαι, ἧς κἂν πολλάκις ἀκούσω" πάλιν ἀχούειν ἐπιθυμῶ, οὐκ ἀχολάστῳ φωνῆς ἡδονῇ ολαιλα ' πάκτ ΒΖΟχΟΙΒΙ0 ΠΟΟΛΑΙΙΑΤΉ, Ἠὲ ἘΔ Βοε στλλολ ὁλΆοΎ ΒΝ πρέώλολθηζ, πἨό ποπεουβέημ μα οὐκ τὰ ταήπλαζ, ἡ πόμεηβ με οὗ τη, μπό ΒΈΙΗΛΤΟ ἃ ΒΑΛΈΡ ἢ «πλοέηϊα. Ποῤίτ)" πὸ σλάφωμ τς οαἦτ, πόραμαια ὁ ἐλληνοβ πα ὑμρμημι, πὖ ποσπες
want to listen to it, not overcome by a disordered passion for song, but instructing myself in the divine mysteries and awaiting as an end, not death, but eternal salvation. For this song is not that of the pagan Sirens who bring death, but it is that of a divine choir of prophets, for whom one does not have to stop the ears of one's companions nor impose chains upon oneself, in fear of the punishment that will follow the hearing. With the Sirens, their listener loses his life at the same time as their song ends; with them, he who does not cease to listen to them will enjoy the better life, guided by the Holy Spirit. The old man of Ithaca, according to the pagan fable, to whom his unbridled passion for song made him desire to hear the Sirens, sailed captive toward Sicily and stopped the ears of his companions; not that he envied them the hearing of this song, not that he himself loved to cover himself with chains, but

poured into human ears, but the complete loosening of all bonds and the deliverance of the hearing in all those who approach. Let them come, for that is the song that it is fitting to hear, these are the singers that I want one to commit to practicing. And if one desires to hear also the choir of the apostles, one will encounter in their song the same accord. For the prophets sang in advance, by mystical way, the economy of divine things, and the song of the apostles is the interpretation of the mystical meaning of their announcing words. O the harmonious accord composed by the Holy Spirit! O the admirable choir of those who sing the mysteries! To their song, I vow to join my own. poured into human ears, but the complete loosening of all bonds and the deliverance of the hearing in all those who approach. Let them come, for that is the song that it is fitting to hear, these are the singers that I want one to commit to practicing. And if one desires to hear also the choir of the apostles, one will encounter in their song the same accord. For the prophets sang in advance, by mystical way, the economy of divine things, and the song of the apostles is the interpretation of the mystical meaning of their announcing words. O the harmonious accord composed by the Holy Spirit! O the admirable choir of those who sing the mysteries! To their song, I vow to join my own.


[p. 730] {#po.22.p730}
Βχοπόϊπχ οὐ πο ἦ πρὶ ταποβαῦπε ΠΆΘΗ, ὦ ΟΛΛῈΒ Ἀχολεμχ' ΠΡ ΈΘΒΑΤΟΜ ὠϊιν, ποΐιμε ἶξα ΟΟΙμΑΤΟ ΠΑΞΑΘῈ ἐτο. Ἦς ΟΥ̓ΚΈΓΛΗ, ὙΛῈεἢ, ΠΈΓΗΗ Ἀογχοπς μπᾶ, ΜῊ 3λκ εὕλη Νὰ ποολογιληϊεῦ οὐ τη μπὲ ἤματη, " Ὁπλοε λα. ἔοτε οκλϑληΐε πάπας πῆτηε. ἦξκ πὸ οὐπο ἡ ΒΑΙΙΒΙΠΗΧ᾽ ΟΑ ἨΛΟΛΑΛΗΤῊ πὴ ὦ ΤῊΧΣ Βερθαλα, ὦ πλὴε ἐσλλ Μη ἔοτα οπηεἦ ΠΒΈΤΒΗΛ ὙΡΑΒΉΜΚΖ, θέμα πὲ ὉΧΈΟΡΖ ΒΑΙΙ, ΠΟΒΗΧΧ ΚΑΠῈ ἦ ς ἩΑΜῊ ΓΛΑΘΑΆΙΒΗΧΧ ἘΟπΕΟΤΒΈΉ μα ταλίημ᾽ πρῊ πηχπτε ὦ ὙΛΑΤΌΛΑΤΗ φφάξαν ὁ, ἄκολε ΜῊ ΒΟῊ ΒΕΞΑΒΗΘΤΆΝΝ ὦ ὙΜΟΤΗ πολλότε ολάχαι, μὲ πολφαπιαύιμε Μλήμόβει ΞΑΈΜΟΤΗ, ἨῊ ἤολπα πολοκμξ Τοραῖμε πράτα, ἨῊ ἠὠοπφόκει χλλάμε πράττα ἰἄκοπε ἔτοσκε ΔΈΛΑ σλλτόλλπις πενλπήλαχα πράτα, πό οὴχ κὐομε οτολημε ἘΟΈΧ᾽ ΤΠ πὶε ποκλο πλὺχ πλήςπηκμἦ
Let us sing then, we too, the same song, and raise our hymn toward the most holy Father, by celebrating according to the Spirit Jesus who is in his bosom. Do not flee, man, the spiritual hymn, and do not refuse to listen. Our song does not give death, it teaches salvation. As for me, I believe I already enjoy the supreme goods by discussing these questions, especially when I have before me such a flowery parterre, I mean the assembly that you form, you who all together listen and sing with me the divine mysteries. With you, it is also without fear that I speak, because you lend me ears pure of all envy. You do not imitate the jealousy of Cain, you do not persecute your brother on the model of Esau, you do not approve of the brothers of Joseph for having hated him for the words he spoke: all these bad sentiments are far from you, given that each of us considers himself only as the spokesperson of his neighbor; and that is why there is
ὁποέτο ΜΗΜΤᾺ ΟΑ ΤΛΑτολᾶ, ἦ τοῦ ΔΈΛΑ ΦΕΒἨΟΟΤΗ 3ΞΛῈ ΜΈΟΤΕ ἨλΛΟΖ, ΜΕΠόΟΤΉΓΧ ἈΡΑΤΊΑ ΝΑΒΟΝΒΉΛΒΑΤΗ ἩΛΟΥΜΗΚΊΠΕΙΙ οα. ὐλε ποολϑιπλεῖοῖ Αρερᾶ ἦ οχπορϑ Ἱεοτηλ ἦ Λογχόβηομ ὁ ἘφΛΙΠΗΟΥ" ΟΖ ΟΗΠΗΜῊ ΠΡΗΘΝ ἘΚΙΤῊ ΜΟΛΗχλ.
no wicked envy in you, accustomed as you are to compensate for the imperfections of your brother. O the beautiful audience, the august banquet, the rich spiritual food! It is in such a society that I vowed, with reason, to always live.

Ἴλσωμεν τοιγαροῦν καὶ ἡμεῖς τὴν ὁμοίαν ᾧδήν, καὶ τὸν ὕμνον ἀναπέμψωμεν τῷ ἁγίῳ πατρί, πνεύματι' δοξάζοντες Ἰησοῦν τὸν ἐν χόλποις αὐτοῦ. By considering this spectacle, I began to observe the sky, and I wanted to measure in my spirit its sphere: where does its axis begin and where does it end, I asked myself, what is its movement, if it is the movement of translation, that is to say from one place to another, or the circular movement, and how does it have its fixed center. But the sun also called for my research: what is its mode of position in the sky, what is the revolution of its course, and what are its progressive displacements on the ecliptic; even while moving thus, it does not deviate from its route, but it too obeys, one can say, the instruction of a superior, and it shines among us in the time that it is permitted to do so, then it goes away as if it were recalled. By considering this spectacle, I began to observe the sky, and I wanted to measure in my spirit its sphere: where does its axis begin and where does it end, I asked myself, what is its movement, if it is the movement of translation, that is to say from one place to another, or the circular movement, and how does it have its fixed center. But the sun also called for my research: what is its mode of position in the sky, what is the revolution of its course, and what are its progressive displacements on the ecliptic; even while moving thus, it does not deviate from its route, but it too obeys, one can say, the instruction of a superior, and it shines among us in the time that it is permitted to do so, then it goes away as if it were recalled.

I was thus led to believe in the existence of a kind of divine economy and of superior forces that maintain the universe, of a power that

Τὸ γὰρ ἐξ οὐκ ὄντων᾽ πεποιηκέναι" οὐδὲ σχοπεῖν “οι καλῶς" ἐδόχει, τέλεον '" ἀπιθάνου “τοῖς πολλοῖς". " ὑπάρχοντος τοῦ λόγου" τὰ γὰρ γινόμενα ἐξ ὄντων πέφυκε τὴν σύστασιν ἔχειν. Καὶ ὁμοίως "ἢ ἀληθῶς ἐδόκει μηδὲν λέγειν ἀεὶ συνεῖναι τῷ θεῷ ἕτερόν τι παρ᾽ αὐτό, ""ς ἀλλ᾽ ἐξ αὐτοῦ «“χαὶ μόνου: '" τὰ ὄντα γεγονέναι" καὶ γάρ πως ἔπειθέ μ: τὸ εὔτακτον τῶν στοιχείων καὶ ἡ περὶ αὐτὰ τῆς φύσεως εὐκοσμία. ἣε ογοτλέτα πε μα ὦ ἬΧΖ ἐλημῈχ οι, Ἡχ' κα τόπα ὠταλλϑῦ οἡ ΒΟΑ ὁΖοτλζ ἤπογτα, ἧμια ἦλαχζ, πὲ ΩΤ ποέτο ΔῊ ΟΟΥΒΑΤΟ ΟΖ ΒΌΓΟΜΕ ΠΡΗΘΝΟ ἤλη ὧτ ὁλμοτο ἐλήηοτγο, λιμὸν ὁ ΠΗΗχ ΝΗμοαδε. ἐπε ἘῸ ὩΤ Ἦξὲ ΟΟΥΒΗΧ ὙΤΟ ὈΧΤΕΘΦΗΤΗ ΜῊ ΟἸΜΟΤΦΗΤῊ ΜῊ ἘΛό ἈΘΕΡῈ ΜΉ ΑΙΠΕ ὁ, ἨῊ Ζαφάιπεη κε ολοΒ δ ΜΒΟΓΗΠΙΖ ΟΟὙΙμ αι ΜΕ ἘΡΗΟΥ ΒΜΒΑΟΙΒΑΙΑΪ ἘῸ ΟἹ ΟΑΙΒΗΧ ΒΛΈΜΚΟΙΙΑ ΟΧΟΤΑΆΛΟΗΤΕ ἀμέτη. ἢ πηξ πὸ ἀπὸ πὸ ἀοτηπὲ ππᾶπς τὰς πηέρομϑαε ἘΕΙΤῊ ΥὙΛΑΓΟΔΑΤῊ Ὁ ΒΟΓΟΜ ΠΡΗΘΝΟ ἰ, φέκιμε! ΑφΟΥΤΟμΥ ἠνλκόμον ἰὰ πὲ ἰἄκοπε ὦνχ], πό ὡτ ἐλμηόγο ὦμογο ΟΊ μΉ ΙΖ ἘΜΤΗ ΝΈΚΑΚΟ πὸ ὙήῊΧ ΟΥ̓ΒΈΡΑΛΙΕἢ ΙΔ ΟΖΟΤΑΒΧΝΜΗ ὦ πλλτόε ἦχζ οὐκφαπιεηῖε ὀότεοτπλ.
My thought did not stop at these questions alone: I now undertook to seek the principle of the constitution of this universe, whether it draws its origin from something that coexisted eternally with God or from God alone, without anything coexisting with him. Indeed, that God created it from nothing is a hypothesis that it did not seem to me even appropriate to discuss, for it is judged completely implausible by most philosophers: it is a law that nothing comes from nothing. And yet it seemed to me equally true to admit that nothing coexisted eternally with God different from him, and that existing things draw their origin from God alone: this conviction was in a way imposed upon me by the order of the parts of the universe and their happy natural disposition. My thought did not stop at these questions alone: I now undertook to seek the principle of the constitution of this universe, whether it draws its origin from something that coexisted eternally with God or from God alone, without anything coexisting with him. Indeed, that God created it from nothing is a hypothesis that it did not seem to me even appropriate to discuss, for it is judged completely implausible by most philosophers: it is a law that nothing comes from nothing. And yet it seemed to me equally true to admit that nothing coexisted eternally with God different from him, and that existing things draw their origin from God alone: this conviction was in a way imposed upon me by the order of the parts of the universe and their happy natural disposition.

Quite satisfied with this way of seeing things, I returned home. The next day, that is to say today, I saw on my way two beings of the same race, that is to say two men, who were quarreling and insulting each other, then another who was striving to rob his neighbor. Here are others now who were engaging in even more terrible enterprises. One was stripping a dead man and bringing back to the light the corpse already hidden in the earth, and he was outraging this body, image of his own, by abandoning it to the

dogs as pasture. Another drew his sword and marched <as an enemy> against a man, his fellow. The latter sought his salvation in flight, but his adversary did not cease to pursue him and refused to master his fury. In short, he advanced upon him and immediately brandished his sword. The wretch began to implore this enemy who was his fellow, he stretched out suppliant hands toward him, and he consented to give his clothes, asking only to have his life saved. But the other, without restraining his fury in any way, without pity for his neighbor, not wanting to recognize his own image in him, began like a wild beast to tear his prey with the sword, and already he was making it bite into this body like his own: such was the excess of his rage. And one could see the victim who was already lying on the ground, and the assassin who was now stripping his corpse, and who did not even cover with earth the naked body of the one he had stripped of his clothes.

ΤΠ πε πεολόνπερ
Witness to so many crimes, I undertook to seek their origin: I asked myself what is their driving principle, what being invented all this evil among men, where they discovered it, and who taught it to them. To give God as its creator, I did not feel the audacity to do so, even less to admit that it has its substance or its realization in God. How indeed to conceive these ideas of God? He is good, he is the author of good, he has no connection with evil; on the contrary, he cannot by his nature love anything that resembles evil, he forbids it to be born, he rejects those who take pleasure in it and welcomes those who flee from it. And how would it not be absurd to make God the creator of evil, he who reproves it? He would not want one to prevent it from being, if he were himself originally its author: for he wants those who come to him to be his imitators. It seemed to me therefore unreasonable to attribute evil to God, either as issuing from his

essence, or - if one must concede that something can be born from nothing - by saying that God the creator also created evil. For he who would have brought it out of nothing would not make it return there subsequently. Or otherwise,

one must admit that there was a time when God took pleasure in evil, while he no longer takes pleasure in it now: a hypothesis that it seems impossible to me to formulate regarding God, this change that one would attribute to him being contrary to his nature. Witness to so many crimes, I undertook to seek their origin: I asked myself what is their driving principle, what being invented all this evil among men, where they discovered it, and who taught it to them. And how would it not be absurd to make God the creator of evil, he who reproves it? He would not want one to prevent it from being, if he were himself originally its author: for he wants those who come to him to be his imitators. Ἀποίου τε γὰρ καὶ ἀσχηματίστου οὔσης αὐτῆς, πρὸς δὲ τούτοις καὶ ἀτάκτως φερομένης, δεομένης τε τῆς τοῦ θεοῦ τέχνης, οὐκ ἐφθόνησεν οὗτος οὔτε διὰ παντὸς καταλιπεῖν" αὐτὴν οὕτως φέρεσθαι, ἀλλὰ δημιουργεῖν ἤρχετο καὶ ἀπὸ τῶν χειρίστων αὐτῆς τὰ κάλλιστα διαχρίνειν
parts of matter that it was fitting for a God to model. As for the parts that constituted, so to speak, the dregs, he left them in their primitive state, as unfit to be worked and unworthy of him: that is, in my opinion, the source from which evil flows into humanity. For these reasons, I believed I had to admit that something coexisted with God, what is called matter, from which his skillful art made the universe by separating the elements and ordering it in a happy way: it is from this matter that evil must draw its origin. Without qualities and without form, and furthermore agitated by a disordered movement, it demanded the industry of God. Ἀρέπμαζ, με ἡ πλὴὲ
M (the Orthodox). - I approve of your zeal, my friend, and praise your love for philosophical discussion; and that you have admitted, in your explanation of the universe, the existence of a primitive substance that God would have ordered to make the world, I could not blame you for it entirely. It is indeed true that the fact of the production of evil has led most philosophers to the same theory. Indeed, before you and before me, many competent men have treated this question as thoroughly as possible. And some have adopted the same way of seeing as you, others on the contrary have declared God the creator of evil, because they did not dare to posit the existence of a substance coeternal with him, just as the former wanted to admit matter coeternal with God because they did not dare to make God the author of evil: for the ones as for the others, it was a consequence, drawn wrongly through ignorance of the truth, from the fear of slandering the divinity. There are others who have radically rejected any research on this subject, estimating that such a study led to nothing. But for me, our friendship forbids me - everything is common between friends - to refuse the discussion of the problem, and especially when you

into your reasoning. For it is necessary, by all necessity, to say one of two things: that between God and matter there is separation, or else indivision. Now, if one declares for union, one affirms that the uncreated is one: each of them will be a part of the whole of the other, and being mutually parts of the same whole, they will not constitute two uncreated beings, but a single one, composed of diverse parts. The body of man is also formed of different members, and we do not break up the human creature into several beings; if we say on the contrary, as reason requires, that man is a unique being that God created composed of several parts, it is also necessary, if God is not separated from matter, to say that there is only one uncreated being.

ΠῸ πρά Ὲ πε οὗκὸ ἦ τὲ γέκογ, ἠοπόλη απιε ΔῊ
But, it will be said, there is still a third possible thesis, which provides a suitable solution to the problem of the two uncreated beings: one can claim that between God and matter there was neither separation, nor union as between the parts of a whole, and that God was in matter conceived as place, or else that matter was in God. Let us see what will follow. If we say that matter was the place of God, we must necessarily say at the same time that God was limited by a container and that he was circumscribed by matter. On the other hand, he must have been agitated like it by a disordered movement, without knowing immobility or permanence, since his container moved now to one side, now to the other. Furthermore, we are forced also to admit that God resided in a much inferior medium: if it is true that matter was originally confused and that he ordered it with the intention of bringing it to a better state, there was a


[p. 751] {#po.22.p751}
ΒΕΒΝ ἘΟΓΧ ἠλὴ ἼΛΟΤῊ ποεῇ πξάιπε εἴ. ἀψε ΒΟ Κα ἸΆΘΤΗ ποεῆ φέμη χόμμετ τὸ ΒοΓᾺ, πΗόϑ Ἐπὶ ΠΑῚΣ ΜΑΛΈΠΙΠΑ ΒΕΙΒΗ ΥὙΛΑΤΌΛΕΤΙ, ἐτο, ΠῈ ἡ ἰἀκοπεῖ" ἀλοτα εὰ ΒΟΕΓΟ ΒΗΈΘΤΗ ΒΟΓΆ. ἄμε ΔῊ ΒΑ ΒΟῊ ΓΛΑΓΌΛΕΤΕ ΒΜΙΤῊ ἦ ἘΧ ΒΟ ΟΑ ΒΙΙΈΘΟΤΗΤΗ ΒΕΙΒΕν, ΚΑΚῸ Ὁ ΤῈ ὈΧΤΕΟΡΗΛΑ ΔΑ ὈΚΛΊΚΕΤΕ ΤΟ ἨδγπΑ ΕΟ ἐςτε ΟΣΠφΑΥλΗΐς κὸ8 Ἂν δ᾽ ἄρα τις χαὶ τρίτον" εἶναι λέξη λόγον ὡς" ἁρμόζοντα περὶ τῶν ἀγενήτων λέγεσθαι", τουτέστιν τὸ μὴ χεχωρίσθαι τὸν θεὸν τῆς ὕλης μηδ᾽ ἡ αὖ πάλιν ὡς μέρη
time when God found himself in the midst of confusion. I would still have the right to ask if God occupied the whole place of matter or if he occupied only a part of it. If one wants him to have occupied only a part of it, one affirms most categorically in the world that he is lesser than matter, since he was contained entirely in one of its parts. But if one claims that he occupied all of matter and that he spread throughout its entire extent, let one then explain
ἡνῶσθαι, εἶναι δὲ χαθάπερ ἐν τόπῳ τῇ ὕλῃ" τὸν θεὸν ἢ " χαὶ τὴν ὕλην ἐν τῷ θεῷ, τὸ συνέχον 'δ ἀχουέτω: ὅτι, ἐὰν τόπον τοῦ θεοῦ τὴν ὕλην εἴπωμεν, ἐξ ἀνάγχης αὐτὸν καὶ χωρητὸν λέγειν δεῖ"" χαὶ πρὸς τῆς ὕλης περιγραρόμενον. Ἀλλὰ μὴν καὶ ὁμοίως αὐτὸν τῇ ὕλῃ ἀτάκτως φέρεσθαι δεῖ, μὴ ἵστασθαι δὲ μηδὲ μένειν αὐτὸν ἐφ᾽ ἑαυτοῦ ἀνάγχη, τοῦ ἐν ᾧ ἐστιν ἄλλοτ᾽ ἄλλοσε' "φερομένου. Πρὸς δὲ τούτοις καὶ ἐν χείροσι γεγονέναι τὸν θεὸν εἰπεῖν ἀνάγκη" εἰ γάρ ποτε ἄκοσμος'" ἦν ἡ ὕχη, ἐκόσμησεν δὲ αὐτὴν εἰς τὸ κρεῖττον τρέψαι προαιρούμενος, ἦν ποτε ὅτ᾽ ἐν ἀχοσμήτοις'" ἦν ὁ θεός. Δικαίως δ᾽ ἂν καὶ τοῦτο ἐροίμην '", πότερον ἐπλήρου τὴν ὕχην ὁ θεὸς ἢ ἐν μέρει τινὶ ὑπῆρχεν αὐτῆς. Εἰ μὲν γὰρ ἐν μέρει τινὶ τῆς ὕλης εἰϊτεῖν τις ἐθέλοι τὸν θεόν, πλεῖστον ὅσον μικρότερον αὐτὸν τῆς ὕλης λέγει, εἴ γε' δὴ μέρος αὐτῆς ὅλον ἐχώρησεν τὸν θεόν. Εἰ δὲ ἐν πάσῃ εἶνα: λέγοι χαὶ δι’ ὅλης χεχωρηκέναι τῆς ὕλης ', πῶς ταύτην ἐδημιούργησεν φοασάτω ᾽"" ἀνάγκη γὰρ ἢ" συστολήν τινα τοῦ θεοῦ λέγειν, ἧς γενο- ΤΛΑΤΌΔΆΤΗ Ἀοπῖε, ἐμπογῆκε ἈΒΜΙΒιμον ΤΟ ΆΠΙς ὧπο ὧτ πετοε τῆλε, ἤλὴ ἡ οεπε οΖ Βέιμειν ὙΒΟΡΉ ΤῊ πὲ ἀπϑηϊλ ὠτιπε τα ᾿πξοτλ.
how he could have ordered it. One must admit, either that God contracted himself and that after this contraction he ordered the part from which he had withdrawn, or else that, lacking the space to retreat, he exercised his ordering activity upon himself at the same time as upon matter. But, it will be said, there is still a third possible thesis, which provides a suitable solution to the problem of the two uncreated beings: one can claim that between God and matter there was neither separation, nor union as between the parts of a whole, and that God was in matter conceived as place, or else that matter was in God. Let us see what will follow. If we say that matter was the place of God, we must necessarily say at the same time that God was limited by a container and that he was circumscribed by matter. On the other hand, he must have been agitated like it by a disordered movement, without knowing immobility or permanence, since his container moved now to one side, now to the other. Furthermore, we are forced also to admit that God resided in a much inferior medium: if it is true that matter was originally confused and that he ordered it with the intention of bringing it to a better state, there was a

If one says that it is matter that was in God, one must also examine the two ways of understanding it: is it claimed that God was discontinuous and that matter was in him as are animal species in the air, which moves aside and divides to receive what is born in its midst, or else that matter was in God as in a place, that is to say like water in the earth? If we answer: like animals in the air, we must necessarily say that God was divided. If we answer: like water in the earth, since matter was in disorder and confusion, and that furthermore it carried evil within it, we are forced to say of God that he was the place of confusion and evil. An affirmation which seems to me little respectful toward the divinity, and even more perilous for the reasoning: you postulate indeed the existence of matter to escape the obligation of

εἰς ὑποδοχὴν τῶν γινομένων ἐν αὐτῷ", ἢ ὡς ἐν τόπῳ, τουτέστιν ὥσπερ ἐν γῇ ὕδωρ. Εἰ μὲν γὰρ εἴποιμεν' ὡς ἐν ἀέρι, μεριστὸν ἀνάγκη τὸν θεὸν εἰπεῖν. making God the author of evil, and here your fine intention leads you to make him the receptacle of evil. Ἐπεὶ δὲ τὴν τῶν κακῶν γένεσιν αἰτίαν ἔφησθα" εἶναι τῆς τοιαύτης ὑπονοίας, διὰ τοῦτο ἐπὶ τὴν ἐξέτασιν τούτων ἔρχεσθαί μοι δοκῶ "5" φανεροῦ γὰρ γινομένου τοῦ λόγου "' καθ᾽ ὃν τρόπον ἐστὶ τὰ χακά, καὶ ὅτι οὐχ οἷόν τέ ἐστιν ἀναίτιον τῶν χαχῶν εἰπεῖν τὸν θεὸν ἐκ τοῦ ὕλην αὐτῷ ὑποτιθέναι, τὴν τοιαύτην ὑπόνοιαν ἀναιρεῖσθαί μοι δοχεῖ '". Εὶ μὲν οὖν τὴν ὕλην ἐκ τῶν ὑποστάντων γενητῶν ὑπονοῶν᾽ ἀγένητον ὑπάρχειν ἔλεγες, πολὺν ἂν περὶ ταύτης" ἐποιησάμην λόγον εἰς ἀπόδειξιν τοῦ ὅτι ἀδύνατον ὑπάρχειν αὐτὴν ἀγένητον. Ἐπεὶ δὲ τὴν τῶν κακῶν γένεσιν αἰτίαν ἔφησθα" εἶναι τῆς τοιαύτης ὑπονοίας, διὰ τοῦτο ἐπὶ τὴν ἐξέτασιν τούτων ἔρχεσθαί μοι δοκῶ "5" φανεροῦ γὰρ γινομένου τοῦ λόγου "' καθ᾽ ὃν τρόπον ἐστὶ τὰ χακά, καὶ ὅτι οὐχ οἷόν τέ ἐστιν ἀναίτιον τῶν χαχῶν εἰπεῖν τὸν θεὸν ἐκ τοῦ ὕλην αὐτῷ ὑποτιθέναι, τὴν τοιαύτην ὑπόνοιαν ἀναιρεῖσθαί μοι δοκεῖ "ἢ,
If it was from the existence of created things that you had drawn your hypothesis of uncreated matter, I would have undertaken an extended discussion of the problem of matter to demonstrate that it could not be uncreated. But since you declared just a moment ago that it is the fact of the production of evil that is the reason for this thesis, it is to the examination of the question of evil that I decide to pass. I want to specify in what manner evil exists and to establish that by placing an uncreated matter alongside God one does not succeed in clearing God of the responsibility for evil: if I succeed in this, your hypothesis falls, it seems to me. If it was from the existence of created things that you had drawn your hypothesis of uncreated matter, I would have undertaken an extended discussion of the problem of matter to demonstrate that it could not be uncreated. But since you declared just a moment ago that it is the fact of the production of evil that is the reason for this thesis, it is to the examination of the question of evil that I decide to pass. I want to specify in what manner evil exists and to establish that by placing an uncreated matter alongside God one does not succeed in clearing God of the responsibility for evil: if I succeed in this, your hypothesis falls, it seems to me.


[p. 755] {#po.22.p755}
Φὴς τοίνυν ἄποιον ὕλην συνυπάρχειν τῷ θεῷ, ἐξ ἧς τὴν τοῦ κόσμου γένεσιν ἐδημιούρ- γήσεν; "Οὕτω μοι δοκεῖ. "Οὐκοῦν εἰ ἄποιος ἐτύγχανεν ἡ ὕλη, γέγονεν δὲ χόσμος πρὸς τοῦ θεοῦ, ἐν δὲ τῷ κόσμῳ αἱ ποιότητες, τῶν ποιοτήτων γέγονεν ποιητὴς ὁ θεός; Οὕτως ἔχει. Ἐπεὶ δέ σου χαὶ λέγοντος πρόσθεν ἤκουον ὡς ἀδύνατον ἐξ οὐκ ὄντων γίνεσθαί τι, πρὸς τὴν ἐρώτησιν ἀπόκριναι τὴν ἐμήν" δοκεῖ σοι τὰς τοῦ χόσμου ποιότητας μὴ ἐξ ὑποχει- μένων ποιοτήτων γεγονέναι; !
You affirm then that there was matter devoid of qualities that coexisted with God, and that God ordered it to produce the world? A. - That is what I think. M. - Well, if matter was without qualities, if the ordering of the world is the work of God, if qualities appear in the world, God was the creator of the qualities? A. - That is it. M. - But as I heard you say previously that it is impossible to create anything from nothing, I beg you to answer my question: do you admit that the qualities that the world possesses do not draw their origin from pre-existing qualities?
Φὴς τοίνυν ἄποιον ὕλην συνυπάρχειν τῷ θεῷ, ἐξ ἧς τὴν τοῦ κύσμου γένεσιν ἐδημιούρ- γήσεν; "Οὕτω μοι δοκεῖ. "Οὐκοῦν εἰ ἄποιος ἐτύγχανεν ἡ ὕλη, γέγονεν δὲ κόσμος πρὸς τοῦ θεοῦ, ἐν δὲ τῷ χόσμῳ αἱ ποιότητες, τῶν ποιοτήτων γέγονεν ποιητὴς ὁ θεός; Οὕτως ἔχει. Ἐπεὶ δέ σου καὶ λέγοντος πρόσθεν ἤχουον ὡς ἀδύνατον ἐξ οὐκ ὄντων γίνεσθαί τι, πρὸς τὴν ἐρώτησιν ἀπόκριναι τὴν ἐμήν' δοκεῖ σοι τὰς τοῦ χόσμου ποιότητας μι ἐξ ὑποκει- μένων ποιοτήτων γεγονέναι; Δοκεῖ. Ἕτερον δέ τι παρὰ τὰς οὐσίας ὑπάρχειν αὐτάς; Οὕτως ἔχει. Οὐκοῦν εἰ μήτε ἐξ ὑποχειμένων ποιοτήτων τὰς ποιότητας ἐδημιούργησεν ὃ θεός, μήτε ἐκ τῶν οὐσιῶν ὑπάρχουσιν, τῷ μηδὲ οὐσίας αὐτὰς εἶναι, ἐκ μὴ ὄντων αὐτὰς ὑπὸ τοῦ θεοῦ γεγονέναι ἀνάγκη εἰπεῖν" ὅθεν περιττῶς ἐδόκεις μοι λέγειν ἀδύνατον εἶναι δοξάζειν ἐξ οὐχ ὄντων γεγονέναι τι πρὴς τοῦ θεοῦ. Δοκεῖ. Ἕτερον δέ τι παρὰ τὰς οὐσίας ὑπάρχειν αὐτάς; Οὕτως ἔχει. Οὐκοῦν εἰ μήτε ἐξ ὑποχειμένων ποιοτήτων τὰς ποιότητας ἐδημιούργησεν ὃ θεός, μήτε ἐκ τῶν οὐσιῶν ὑπάρχουσιν, τῷ μηδὲ οὐσίας αὐτὰς εἶναι, ἐκ μὴ ὄντων αὐτὰς ὑπὸ τοῦ θεοῦ γεγονέναι ἀνάγκη εἰπεῖν" ὅθεν περιττῶς ἐδόχεις μοι λέγειν ἀδύνατον εἶναι δοξάζειν ἐξ οὐκ ὄντων γεγονέναι τι πρὸς τοῦ θεοῦ.
You affirm then that there was matter devoid of qualities that coexisted with God, and that God ordered it to produce the world? A. - That is what I think. M. - Well, if matter was without qualities, if the ordering of the world is the work of God, if qualities appear in the world, God was the creator of the qualities? A. - That is it. M. - But as I heard you say previously that it is impossible to create anything from nothing, I beg you to answer my question: do you admit that the qualities that the world possesses do not draw their origin from pre-existing qualities? A. - I admit it. M. - And that they are different from substances? A. - Yes. M. - Well, if on the one hand God did not form the qualities by taking the matter from pre-existing qualities, and if on the other hand they do not issue from substances, not being substances themselves, it must necessarily be said that God drew them from nothing. That is why your declarations on the impossibility of thinking that God created something from nothing seemed to me just now superfluous. A. - I admit it. M. - And that they are different from substances? A. - Yes. M. - Well, if on the one hand God did not form the qualities by taking the matter from pre-existing qualities, and if on the other hand they do not issue from substances, not being substances themselves, it must necessarily be said that God drew them from nothing. That is why your declarations on the impossibility of thinking that God created something from nothing seemed to me just now superfluous.
Ἀλλ᾽ ὁ μὲν περὶ τούτου λόγος ὧδε ἐχέτω. Καὶ γὰρ παρ᾽ ἡμῖν ὁρῶμεν ἀνθρώπους ἐξ οὐκ ὄντων ποιοῦντάς τινα, εἰ χαὶ τὰ μάλιστα δοκοῦσιν οὗτοι ποιεῖν ἔν τινι. Οἷον ἐπὶ τῶν ἀρχιτεκτόνων τὸ παράδειγμα λάθωμεν" χαὶ γὰρ οὗτοι ποιοῦσιν πόλεις οὐχ ἐχ πόλεων καὶ ναοὺς ὁμοίως οὐκ ἐκ ναῶν. Εἰ δ᾽ ὅτι τούτοις οὐσίαι ὑπόκεινται οἴει ἐξ ὄντων αὐτοὺς ταῦτα ποιεῖν, σφάλλῃ τῷ λόγῳ᾽ οὐδὲ γὰρ ἡ οὐσία ἐστὶν ἡ ποιοῦσα τὴν πόλιν ἢ αὖ πάλιν τοὺς νεώς", ἀλλ᾽ ἡ περὶ τὴν οὐσίαν τέχνη" ἡ δὲ τέχνη οὐχ ἐξ ὑποκειμένης τινὸς ἐν ταῖς οὐσίαις τέλνης γίνεται, ἀλλ᾽ ἐξ οὐκ οὔσης ἐν αὐταῖς γίνεται. ᾿Απαντήσειν δέ μοι δοχεῖς οὕτω τῷ ψπῶτ περ οογμηχ ἔπιε ἡ ὀοτμοτπο' ὁμης ἀπ ἜΤΗ ποκλβλέτε οα. ἦ ἔλμα πὲ λθκμ τὴ ἔτει ὦ Ξληχζ λόγῳ᾽ ὅτι ὁ τεχνίτης ἐξ ἧς ἔχει τέχνης τὴν ἐν τῇ οὐσίᾳ τέχνην ποιεῖ. Πρὸς δὲ τοῦτο λέγεσθαι
But let us demonstrate this point first. Do we not see among us men who create things from nothing, even if they seem to create in something? Take for example the case of architects: they build cities, and they do not make them with already existing cities; likewise they construct temples, but not with temples. If, from the fact that substances serve as matter for these creations, you conclude that architects create from something that already is, you are mistaken in your reasoning. For it is not the substance that makes the city or the temples, but the art that is exercised upon the substance; and the work of art is not born from an art pre-existing in the substances, but from an art that does not exist in them. You will object to my argumentation, I imagine, that it is the artisan who creates from the art he has within himself, the art that he puts into the substance. But I have a


[p. 756] {#po.22.p756}


[p. 757] {#po.22.p757}
τοῦτ᾽ εὖ ἔχειν μοι δοχεῖ' ὅτι οὐδὲ ἐν τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ ἔκ τινος ὑποχειμένης τέχνης προσγίνεται. Οὐδὲ γὰρ ἔνεστιν αὐτὴν ἐφ᾽ ἑαυτῆς οὖσαν δοῦναι τὴν τέχνην᾽ τῶν γὰρ συμδεθηκότων ἐστὶ καὶ τῶν τότε τὸ εἶναι λαγχανόντων ὁπόταν ἐν οὐσίχ' γένηται. Ὃ μὲν γὰρ ἄνθρωπος καὶ χωρὶς τῆς ἀρχιτεχτονικῆς ἔσται, ἡ δ᾽ οὐχ ἔσται ἐὰν μιὴ πρότερον ἄνθρωπος ἧ. Ὅθεν τὰς τέχνας ἐξ οὐκ ὄντων εἰς ἀνθρώπους πεφυκέναι γίνεσθαι λέγειν ἀναγκαῖον. Εἰ τοίνυν τοῦτο οὕτως ἔχον ἐπ᾽ ἀνθρώπων ἐδείξαμεν, πῶς οὐχὶ προσῆχε τὸν θεὸν μὴ μόνον ποιότητας ἐξ οὐκ ὄντων φάναι δύνασθαι ποιεῖν, ἀλλὰ καὶ οὐσίας; Τῷ γὰρ δυνατὸν φανῆναι γίνεσθαί τι ἐξ οὐκ ὄντων τὸ καὶ" τὰς οὐσίας οὕτως ἔχειν δείκνυται. Ἀλλ᾽ ὁ μὲν περὶ τούτου λόγος ὧδε ἐχέτω. Καὶ γὰρ παρ’ ἡμῖν ὁρῶμεν ἀνθρώπους ἐξ οὐχ ὄντων ποιοῦντάς τινα, εἰ χαὶ τὰ μάλιστα δοκοῦσιν οὗτοι ποιεῖν ἔν τινι. Οἷον ἐπὶ τῶν
good answer to make to this objection, namely that in man too art does not come from a pre-existing art. It is impossible indeed to claim that art has its existence in itself: it is one of the accidents of substance, and one of those attributes that only arrive at being when they are in a substance. For man will exist even without the art of building, but art will not be if a man does not exist first. One is therefore obliged to say of the arts that their nature is to come to be in men by being born from nothing. Well, if we have established that it is so in the case of men, how would it be fitting in the case of God to deny that he could have created from nothing, not only the qualities, but also the substances? Indeed, from the moment that it appears possible that something is born from nothing, the demonstration holds equally for the substances.

Οὐδὲ γὰρ ἔνεστιν αὐτὴν ἐφ᾽ ἑαυτῆς οὖσαν δοῦναι τὴν τέχνην᾽ τῶν γὰρ συμδεθηκότων ἐστὶ καὶ τῶν τότε τὸ εἶναι λαγχανόντων ὁπόταν ἐν οὐσίχ' γένηται. questions. Is evil for you a substance, or a quality of substance? A. - A quality of substance, I think you say well. M. - And was matter originally without qualities and without form? [p. 758] {#po.22.p758}
με πε ἡπλίπη κὶ οπαΣ ὙΤὃ ΓΛΑΤΟΛΙΙμΕ, ἩΛΎΒΗΛΗ ὁλοπο. ψε πὲ τ πρξηϊα ΛΗ ἈΞήομλΗΪε' ΕΠ ἘΜΛῸ, πὸ ἘΜ πτορόε ὦ Ξλμχ Ὑτὸ δοτλλαλχ' ηά ἔλμα πὸ φάπεμ ἈἙΔΑ πλὰς ὦ πολϑα ἢ ἘΛΗΜΕΧ" ΔΈΛΑ ΟΧΟΤΑΆΛΕΝΪΕ ΤΕΟΡΗΙΙΣ ὁδὸς Βεοχ, «ΟΡ με ΠΡΑΙΒΑΤῊ ὦ ΟῊΧ ΟΥ̓ΟΤΑΆΛΑΤῊ ΜΌΛΙ.
If you have any answer to make to my critique, I give you the floor. If our discussion were only an oratorical joust, I would not consent to take up the problem of evil on other bases. But since it is rather the duties of friendship and the desire to be useful to one another that command our debate, I am willing to allow you to go back to the beginning of the argumentation and to pose new premises.


[p. 759] {#po.22.p759}
Εὶ δέ τι πρὸς ταῦτα λέγειν' ἔχεις, ἄρχου τοῦ λόγου. A. - As for my feelings, they have been known to you for a long time, I think: you know that I love serious discussion, and that I do not want to make falsehood triumph by means of specious arguments, but to discover the truth thanks to a rigorous search; and you, for your part, I know well that you think the same. That is why I beg you to choose the method that you judge capable of making you reach the truth, and to follow it in all confidence. There will be an advantage in this not only for you, who will have found the right solution, but also for me and my ignorance. A. - As for my feelings, they have been known to you for a long time, I think: you know that I love serious discussion, and that I do not want to make falsehood triumph by means of specious arguments, but to discover the truth thanks to a rigorous search; and you, for your part, I know well that you think the same. That is why I beg you to choose the method that you judge capable of making you reach the truth, and to follow it in all confidence. There will be an advantage in this not only for you, who will have found the right solution, but also for me and my ignorance.

M. - Well, my dear friend, since you argue that evil is also a substance, we must examine the problem of substance. Is substance, for you, a corporeal essence? A. - Yes. M. - And does corporeal essence exist in itself and not require the existence of another essence to realize it? A. - That is so. The idea must surely come to mind that evil could be a substance. Likewise, I do not see that it exists outside of substances.

M. - But is evil, for you, the act of some acting being? A. - It seems so to me. M. - And are acts only realized if the acting being is there to realize them? A. - That is so. M. - If the acting being is lacking, will there be no product of its action either? A. - No. M. - Well, if substance is a corporeal essence, if corporeal essence does not require the existence of another essence in which to be realized, if evil exists as the act of an acting being, if acts require the existence of an acting being in which they are realized, then evil will not be a substance. If evil is a substance, and if murder is an evil, then murder will be a substance. But in fact, murder is only the act of an acting being: therefore, murder is not a substance.
Οὕτω μοι φαίνεται. Αἱ δὲ ἐνέργειαι τότε τὸ εἶναι λαμόάνουσιν ὁπότ᾽ ἂν ὁ ἐνεργῶν παρῇ; Οὕτως ἔχει. Οὐκ ὄντος δὲ τοῦ ἐνεργοῦντος, οὐδ᾽ ὅπερ ἐνεργεῖ ἔσται ποτέ; Οὐκ ἔσται. Οὐκοῦν" εἰ ἡ οὐσία σωματική τίς ἐστι σύστασις, ἡ δὲ σωματιχὴ σύστασις οὐ δεῖταί τινος ἐν ᾧ γενομένη τὸ εἶναι χήψεται, τὰ δὲ καχὰ ἐνέργειαι ὑπάρχουσί τινος, αἱ δὲ ἐνέρ- γεῖαι δέονταί τινος ἐν ᾧ γενόμεναι τὸ εἶναι λαμβάνουσιν, οὐχ ἔσονται οὐσίαι" τὰ χανά. Εἰ δὲ οὐσίαι τὰ κακά, καχὸν δὲ ὁ φόνος, οὐσία ἔσται ὁ φόνος. Ἀλλὰ μὴν ὁ φόνος ἐνέργεια ὑπάρχει τινός" οὐχ ἔστιν' ἄρα οὐσία ὁ φόνος".

For substance is neither murder nor adultery, or any similar form of evil. Thus, the grammarian takes his name from grammar, the rhetorician from rhetoric, and the physician from medicine, but substance is not the medical art, nor is it rhetoric or grammar, and it receives these qualifiers according to which of its accidents opinion judges to be characteristic, while being different from its accidents; in the same way, I believe that it is from the forms of evil that opinion recognizes that substance derives the names given to it, but that it is none of these forms of evil.

Consider also, I pray you, that if your mind imagines a being different from men that would be the cause of the evil they commit, this being itself, according to how it acts in them and incites them to evil, is only a wicked being by reason of the evil it does. For if one calls it wicked, it is because it is the author of evil; now, the evil that someone commits is not his substance itself, but only the act of his substance, from which he derives the appellation of wicked. than these crimes. If he is nothing other than these crimes, if these crimes have existence only when they are committed and cease to exist as soon as they cease to be committed, and if they are committed by the fact of men, it is men who will be the creators of this wicked being and the cause of its appearance into existence or its disappearance. But if you make these crimes acts of his substance, it is by his actions that he is wicked, not as a substance. We have said that the qualifier of wicked is derived from the accidents of the substance, which are not the substance itself, just as the qualifier of physician is derived from the medical art. Ἐμοὶ γὰρ'' ἡ ὕλη ποιότητας ἀνάρχως ἔχειν δοκεῖ: οὕτως γὰρ καὶ τὰ καχὰ ἐκ τῆς ἀπορροίας αὐτῆς εἶναι λέγω, ἵνα τῶν χαχῶν ὁ μὲν θεὸς ἀνχίτιος ἢ, τούτων δὲ ἀπάντων ὁ ὕλη αἰτία, ΗΖ ἐπε κα Αρότομογ, ὦ αρόγῆκε, ὁλόβὸ Ἀσβόλη Ἐ ΠΗ ΟΖΤΕΟΗΤΗ ΠΉΜΠΙῊ τα. τ πηχᾶς πὸ ΒΑΡΗΒΖ ΠΟΛᾺ ΟΛΟΒΕΘΗ, ὉΤ πηχλῦ ΟΖΒΉΦΑΤΗ ἈΘΕΡῈ ΜΗΈ τὰ πὸ ἠοτηηθ" ἄμμε πεὸ κακόββοτβα βἈλιπε Βεμμ, ὦ ΚΑΚΟΒΒΟΤΈΘΗΖ ὙΒΟΡΕΝ ἐθτι
the author of evil. Therefore, with him, the discussion is closed. But I, I believe that it is false to affirm that matter is devoid of qualities. For it is not permitted to say of any substance that it is without qualities. Likewise, by the very fact that one says it is without qualities, one indicates the quality it possesses, one describes what qualifies matter, and that itself is a sort of quality. That is why, if you please, I pray you to resume the reasoning against me from the beginning: for me, matter is from all eternity endowed with qualities. This thesis allows me to explain evil as an emanation of matter, so that the responsibility for the existence of evil does not fall upon God, but upon matter, the cause of evil in all its forms. B (Second heterodox). - But that is enough for my comrade; I believe, my friend, that you have argued well against him and that, from the premises he had laid down in his reasoning, you have drawn just deductions. It is certain, in effect, that if matter were primitively devoid of qualities, if God is the author of qualities, and if evil is a quality, God will be

ΤῊΝ ἅκε, με ΤῊ Ε ΔΙΘΕῦ, ΟΧ ΤΌΡΗ ὁ ΚΖ ΜΗῈ Αφάπη ὁλοπερεῦ, ἀιμε πο πὲ ἀπξέτη ΚΑΚΟΒΕΟΤῈΧ ΒΕΙΒᾺ ΜΉΗΤΕ ΟΑ ΒΕΝΑΥΑΛΗἘΓ ὁηηὲ κὸ ἦ Ξάμιαχ ὡτ ἠοτλυληϊλ᾽ εὰ ΤΛΆΤΟΛΙ ἈΜΤΉ, ΔᾺ ὸ ΒΑΛΕΤΕ ΞΜ ΒΟΥΖ ΒΗΝΒΝΗΚΧ, ΟΗχ᾽ πὸ ἘΟΈΧΖ πήνα ΒΕ. 113Ὲ πε λπλ ὁ ΠΉΡΤᾺ ἔπε ΓΛΑΓΟΛΆΤῊ πεὸ κακὸς ΒΕΟΤΕΛ ἘΒΙΤῊ ΒΕΙΒΗ Ἠξ πὸ ἔοτε λ᾽ΞἘ7 φέμη ὦ πογοπιλο ΔΙῸ ἐοτ τ ἈῈ ἰάκοκε ἔστε Ἐδὸ ΚΑΚΟΒΒΟΤΆΛ᾽ Νό ΟΥ̓ΕΟ ἦ ΓΛΑΙΌΛΙΟΗΙΕ ἘεῸ ΚΑΚΟΒΒΟΤΕΛ ἈΜΤῊ ΒΑΒΟΆΚΟΤΕΟ εἃ ποκξΈλλοτε, βακὰ ἐῦτε ἨΑΠΗΟΛΑ ΒΕΙμΒ, ἐπε ἐοτε ΚΑΚΟΒΒΟΤΒΑ ὠπρα3ζ. ΤῊΝ ἅκεν, με ΤῊ Ε ΔΙΘΕῦ, ΟΧ ΤΌΡΗ Α΄ ΚΧ ΜΗῈ Αὐάσπη ὁλοπερεῦ, ἀιμε πὸ πὲ ἀπξέτη ΚΑΚΟΒΕΘΤῈΧ ΒΕ ΜΉΗΤΕ ΟΑ ΒΕΝΑΎΛΛΗἘΓ ὁηηὲ κὸ ἦ Ξάμιαχ ὡτ ἠοτλυληϊλ᾽ εὰ ΥΛΑΤΟΛῸ ἘΜΤῊ, ΔΛ δὲ ΒΆΛΕΤ, ΞΑμας ΒΟΥ ΒΠΝΒΗΗΚΧ, ΟΗΧ᾽ πὲ ΒΟΈΧΣ βήνα δέ.
the author of evil. Therefore, with him, the discussion is closed. But I, I believe that it is false to affirm that matter is devoid of qualities. For it is not permitted to say of any substance that it is without qualities. Likewise, by the very fact that one says it is without qualities, one indicates the quality it possesses, one describes what qualifies matter, and that itself is a sort of quality. That is why, if you please, I pray you to resume the reasoning against me from the beginning: for me, matter is from all eternity endowed with qualities. This thesis allows me to explain evil as an emanation of matter, so that the responsibility for the existence of evil does not fall upon God, but upon matter, the cause of evil in all its forms.

M. - I approve of your zeal, my friend, and I praise you for loving serious discussion. It is indeed true that it is a duty for all those who have a love of study not to accept naively and at random the demonstrations that are made to them, but to submit them to severe criticism. Likewise, if the one who provokes the discussion, by positing false premises, has provided his interlocutor with the opportunity to draw the deductions he wanted, the listeners will not feel convinced for all that. But if it seems possible to them to present a good objection, they will be there to do so. For one of two things: either, by listening to this additional debate oriented in the direction they want, they will draw from the discussion all the profit they can draw from it, or they will convince the arguer of error.

Is it a transformation of substances leading to the substitution of other substances different from the first ones for the primitive substances, or do you say that God preserved the original substances, but by modifying their qualities?

" ἀμεῦ ἡ ολϑαητ τὰ ΓΔΑΤΟΛΑΤῊ ὉΤ ΒΑΕ Α ΧΛΈΒΗΝΈ Ἐμτη, ὦ πηχῆϊε ἣξ ΔΞῈ φέμη ἰἄκὸ πὲ ἐϊμὲ καμεηῖς πρβεμβαλῦτι ἐοτεοτβόμι ΧΛΈΒΗΝΑ ἘΜ ΒΗΙε ἢ ΒΑ -τ ΚΑΚΟΒΕΟΤΈΟΝ ΒΟ ΟΔοσεΗϊΑ ΧΛΈΒΗΝΜΕ ρέκϑ Ἐμτη, ΠΡΈΜΈΝΕΝΟΥ ΔῈ ἄπο περαϑόμν παπεηϊλ ΒΑΚΟΒΕΟΤΕΟΥ τον οήηε ΜῊ ΟΑ ΜΗΠΤ ἦ ΒΟΤΟΥ, ἐοΎΒΟΤΈΟΥ ΤΦΆΠΑΙΒΟΥ Πρ ΠΈΜΕΝΤΕ Βόε ΚΑΒΟΒΒΟΤΕΧ ἐτὸ, ᾿ζτπόρητη εὖ, πὸ πεαϑχεε ἡ ᾿χϑλληῖε ΜΗΛ ΟὟ ΒΟΓᾺ ΒΕΙΤΗ ΓΛΑΓΌΔΙ.
B. - I do not believe at all that there was a mutation of substances, and it seems absurd to me to posit this thesis. It is a modification of qualities that I admit, and it is in relation to qualities that I affirm that there was an operation of God. It is as if one says, for example: a house made of stones; it is not permitted to claim of these stones that, having become a house, they ceased to be stones in their substance, for it is only by the quality of the composition that the house exists, that is to say by a modification of the primary quality of the stones. In the same way, concerning the action of God, I believe that the substance persisted, but that God operated a modification of its qualities, and it is in this sense that I say that the creation of the current world is the work of God.
Οὔτι' μοι δοκεῖ ἀλλαγήν τινα οὐσιῶν γεγονέναι" χαὶ γὰρ ἄτοπον τοῦτο λέγειν μοι φαίνεται". τροπὴν δέ τινα τῶν ποιοτήτων γεγονέναι φημί, καθ᾽ ἃς δημιουργὸν εἶναι λέγω τὸν θεόν. Καὶ ὥσπερ᾽ εἰ τύχοι λέγειν ἐκ λίθων οἰκίαν γεγονέναι, ἐφ᾽ ὧν οὐχ ἔστιν εἰπεῖν ὡς οὐχέτι λίθοι μένουσι τῇ οὐσίχ οἰκία γενόμενοι οἱ λίθοι τῇ γὰρ ποιότητι τῆς συνθέσεως πὴν οἰκίαν γεγονέναι φημί, τραπείσης δηλονότι τῆς προτέρας τῶν λίθων ποιότητος οὕτω μοι δοκεῖ χαὶ τὸν θεόν, ὑπομενούσης τῆς οὐσίας, τροπήν τινα τῶν ποιοτήτων αὐτῆς πεποιη- χέναι᾽, χαθ᾽ ἣν τὴν τοῦδε" τοῦ κόσμου γένεσιν πρὸς τοῦ θεοῦ γεγονέναι λέγω. πο ῤκε μῆπτα ΜῊ ΓΑ ΠΡΈΜΈΝΕΝΤΙ κοῦ ἐοτμοταζ' ΒΗΤΗ ΒΕΠΟΑΘΕΝΟ πὼ ΜῊ οὲ ἌΜΗΤΕ ΘΑ ΠΟΚΑΞΟΒΑΤΗ ἡ ἐπε ὁηηε ΤΛΑΤόλΆτη. B. - That is the thesis I adopted from the beginning. M. - Well, by saying that God left the quality of evil without change, how do you understand it? Do you admit that he could destroy it like the others, but that he did not want to, or do you consider that he did not have the power? If you say that he could, but that he did not want to, you must agree that he is the one responsible for evil, since he tolerated that evil subsisted when he could have suppressed it. And this all the more so since he had undertaken to order matter: for if he had not occupied himself with matter at all, there would be no reason to ask him to account for his tolerances; but since we see him ordering a part of matter and leaving the other


[p. 772] {#po.22.p772}


[p. 773] {#po.22.p773}
Οὕτως ἄνωθεν εἶχον ἐγώ. Πῶς τοίνυν αὐτὸν τὰς τῶν φαύλων ποιότητας ὡς εἶχον καταλελοιπέναι λέγεις Πότερον δυνάμενον μὲν χἀκείνας ἀνελεῖν, οὐ βουληθέντα δέ, ἢ τὸ δύνασθαι μὴ ἔχοντα, Εἰ μὲν γὰρ δυνάμενον λέξεις, οὐ βουληθέντα δέ, αὐτὸν αἴτιον τούτων εἰπεῖν ἀνάγκη, ὅτι δυνά- μένος ποιῆσαι μὴ εἶναι κακὰ συνεχώρησεν αὐτὰ μένειν" ὡς ἦν, καὶ μάλιστα ὅτε δημιουρ-- γεῖν τὴν ὕλην ἤρξατο. Εἰ γὰρ μηδ᾽ ὅλως ἔμελεν αὐτῷ τῆς ὕλης, οὐκ ἂν αἴτιος ἦν ὧν συνεχώρει᾽ μένειν" ἐπεὶ δὲ μέρος μέν τι αὐτῆς δημιουργεῖ, μέρος δέ τι οὕτως" ἐᾷ δυνάμενος λάχε ἘΕΒΊΤΗ ΜΉΤ ΠΗ οα, ὠοταδήπα ἸΑΟΤΕ ΒΕΔ ΒΗΤῊ 3ΔῈ πα" ΠΑΤΟΥΕΟΥ ΤοΑ ὙλΟτΗ ὦπε ἔοτε ὀχτπόρηλχ. Νό οὔπο ἡ ΞἘλὺ πππόγλὰ πὸ οἘΠ ἸΛΟΤῊ πεπρΑπλμσ ΟΥ̓ΒΟΒΑΤΗ ΜῊ λ΄ ΜΝΗΤᾺ, ὠςΤΑΚΗΚΧ ἼΛΟΤᾺ ΒΕΙΒῊ 3ΔῈ ἘΜΤΗ]' οή πε ΟΧΤΒΟΡΗ ΒΕΙΒῊ ἸΔΟΤᾺ, πόμομη εἴ πιΗ 3Ξᾶλα. ὧψε ἘῸ κτὸ ἠοπμιταότι. σἘλὸ πεμη, τοριλα ὠκῥοτη ἧματι. ἩΜΗῈ ποοτραλλπιιῦ πές, πλῚς ἈΡΈΒΗΑΤΟ ἨφΟΥΑΡΑΙποΗἴΑ. πε οὗ Βὸ εἴ ραΑοτροἐμἙ Ἐμππια, ἐπε ἦ πὲ ΠΟῪΘΤΗ ΕΣ Ω πΈλιπε εἴ" ἩΜμΝῈ πὲ πλιλπλο ἸΑοτΗΗ ἡ εὰ ἀνδοτποῦ πρτεμίλ)ετε ἡ Ξάμιχχ. ἤ ὦ ὙΔὴἘ Ἂπὲ ΜῊ ΟΚΑΞληΪΕ ος πρίμαα" ε ΟΥ̓ Ἐὸ πχὠπράπενου ἐμ ἨῊ ἘΜΒΟΤΟΥ ἘΜΆΠΙΒ ὙΒΟΡΑΎΕΙΟ ΧΗΤΡΘΟΤΊΙ, ἐπε πηνοότοπιεῦ ἣξ ΠΟῚΤΗ ΞλΉΧΧ ΟἹ ἐοτμοταλ ἀπΈλμιε᾽ ηχ πλεῦ ΩΤ ἘΟΤᾺ λῈ1 Ἐπ, ἡ Ὑοήοτοῦ πρΗπλΗ πα ιματο τὰ 3ζλ πρίλῦ. ἦι οε, ἐπε πὰ ἘλΑτΟΛΈΤα, ΒΕΒΗ ΟὟ ΕΟΓᾺ ΓΛΑΤΌΔΕΙΠῊ ἈΜΤΉ, ὠκρ Ἀταέτ οὰ πλὰὲ Νὰ τόριπες πρμεμιπλα ' εἥ.
thus abandoned when he could have improved it as well, it seems to me that he deserves to be held responsible, from the fact that he let a bad part subsist for the loss of the part he had ordered. I will say more: the most serious of prejudices was caused to this part of matter, the very one that God arranged, which now experiences evil. Indeed, if one goes to the bottom of things, one will find that the current situation of matter is worse than its primitive disorder. At the time of the original confusion, there was also no perception of evil; now, each of the differentiated parts of matter receives the faculty of experiencing evil. Take for me for example the case of man: as long as the art of the creator had not given him a form and had not made him a living being, his nature entailed complete insensitivity to evil; as soon as he is made man by God, he acquires at the same time the perception of evil that comes to reach him. Thus, this operation of God which, according to you, was conducted for the good of matter, one discovers that it is rather to its detriment that it was exercised upon it. B. - That is the thesis I adopted from the beginning. M. - Well, by saying that God left the quality of evil without change, how do you understand it? Do you admit that he could destroy it like the others, but that he did not want to, or do you consider that he did not have the power? If you say that he could, but that he did not want to, you must agree that he is the one responsible for evil, since he tolerated that evil subsisted when he could have suppressed it. And this all the more so since he had undertaken to order matter: for if he had not occupied himself with matter at all, there would be no reason to ask him to account for his tolerances; but since we see him ordering a part of matter and leaving the other
κἀκεῖνο τρέπειν εἰς τὸ χρεῖττον, αἰτίαν ὀφλισκάνειν ἄξιος εἶναί μοι δοκεῖ, χαταλιπὼν μέρος ὕλης εἶναι πονηρὸν ἐπ᾽ ὀλέθρῳ οὗ ἐδημιούργησε μέρους. Ἀλλὰ μὴν χαὶ τὰ μέγιστα κατὰ τοῦτο τὸ μέρος ἠδικῆσθαί μοι δοκεῖ', τοῦθ᾽ ὅπερ κατεσκεύασε τῆς ὕλης μέρος, ἀντιλαμόα- νόμενον τανῦν τῶν καχῶν. Εἰ γάρ τις ἐξετάζοι ἐπ᾽ ἀκριδὲς τὰ πράγματα, χαλεπώτερον" νῦν εὑρήσει τὴν ὕλην πεπονθυῖαν τῆς προτέρας ἀκοσμίας. Ποὶν γὰρ αὐτὴν διακριθῆναι τὸ μηδὲ αἰσθέσθαι τῶν καχῶν παρῆν αὐτῇ" νυνὶ δὲ ἔχαστον τῶν μερῶν αὐτῆς αἴσθησιν λαμθάνει τῶν καχῶν. ἸΚ αἱ μοι ἐπ᾽ ἀνθρώπου τὸ παράδειγμα" λάδε" πρὶν γὰρ εἰκονισθῇ καὶ ζῷον γένηται τῇ τοῦ δημιουργοῦ τέχνη, τὸ μηδενὸς ἀντιλαμδάνεσθαι τῶν καχῶν παρὰ τῆς φύσεως εἶχεν’ ἀφ᾽ οὗ᾽ δὲ πρὸς τοῦ θεοῦ ἄνθρωπος γίνεται, καὶ τὴν αἴσϑησιν τοῦ προσπελάζοντος χαχοῦ προσλαμθάνει. But if you admit, to explain the persistence of evil, that God could not destroy it, you are led to say of God that he is powerless. This powerlessness can only be understood in two ways: either God is infirm by nature, or he fears disobeying some master to whom he is subject. But to dare to speak of an infirmity of the divine nature is an impiety that puts your very life in peril. And to say that God fears disobeying some more powerful master is to claim that evil is more powerful than him, since it commands the will of God. Which is an absurdity:
ἀδύνατον τὸν θεὸν φήσεις" ὑπάρχειν. Τὸ δὲ ἀδύνατον ἤτοι" τῷ φύσει'" ἀσθενῆ ὑπάρχειν αὐτὸν ἔσται, ἢ τῷ νιχᾶσθαι τῷ φόδῳ δεδουλωμένον πρός τινος χρείττονος. Ἐΐ μὲν οὖν τὸν θεὸν φύσει ἀσθενῆ ὄντα τολμήσεις εἰπεῖν, περὶ τῆς σωτηρίας αὐτῆς κινδυνεύειν "' μοι δοκεῖς.
for then why would it not be evil that would be God, when it has the power, according to your reasoning, to command God? If at least we define God as the being who has authority over all things.

composed to be constituted of a single simple thing. For the term composite indicates the mixture of several simple elements. If you want to claim on the contrary that matter was composite, you affirm at the same time that it resulted from the composition of simple elements. If it is so, these simple elements that composed matter existed first in themselves, which demonstrates that matter is created. Indeed, if matter was a composite, and if composites are constituted of simple elements, there was a time when matter did not yet exist, namely prior to the composition of the simple elements. But if there was a time when matter did not exist, and if there was never a moment when the uncreated did not exist

Do you admit that nothing that exists is its own opposite? B. - I admit it. M. - But is water the opposite of fire? B. - It seems so to me. M. - Likewise is darkness the opposite of light, and hot the opposite of cold, and also moist the opposite of dry? B. - I think so. M. - Well, if none of the elements of reality is its own opposite, and if these elements of reality are opposite to one another, they will not constitute a single matter, and will not be derived from a single matter either. Let me submit to you yet another analogous reasoning. Do you admit that the parts of the same whole do not destroy each other? B. - I admit it. M. - And that fire, water, etc. are parts of matter? B. - I think so. M. - But what? Do you not admit that water destroys fire, light destroys darkness, etc.? B. - Yes.

M. - Well, if the parts of the same whole do not destroy each other, and if these elements destroy one another, they will not be parts of the same whole; if they are not parts of the same whole, they will not be parts of a single matter. Furthermore, these elements will not constitute matter, since nothing that exists destroys itself, by virtue of the law of opposites. Indeed, nothing is its own opposite: opposites are opposed by nature to something that is other than themselves. For example, white is not its own opposite: it is in relation to black that one calls it an opposite. One demonstrates the

same for light, that it is not opposed to itself, but to darkness; and so on. Well, if there existed in addition to the elements a single matter, it would not oppose itself; since opposite elements oppose each other, it is proven that matter does not exist.

to know if they constitute species of a genus or parts of a whole that would be Evil. Indeed: if you admit that they are species of evil, there is no evil in itself different from its manifestations, from the fact that one only observes genera in their species. For example, the animal genus is stated as an attribute of certain species, namely of man and similar beings, and of everything that is animate and endowed with spontaneous movement, but it does not appear as a reality. If we say that this genus exists without claiming that it exists in itself outside of its species, one must likewise say of evil, of which we have made a genus, that it has its existence in its species, but that it is nothing else in itself. If it is so, evil will be created, for its species: murder, adultery, etc., appear created. But as I think you are awaiting the examination of the problem of evil, let us stop there with the question of matter. I wanted to show that matter did not exist, and I consider that I have said enough on this subject. We must now pass to the study of evil, and our research will necessarily bear on human facts. equally created, since the whole is a composite of parts, and the whole will not exist without the parts, while the parts will exist even in the absence of the whole. One does not find in reality a whole of which one part is created and another uncreated. But I consent to consider this hypothesis: we will say that there was a moment when evil existed, but incomplete, namely before God had ordered matter. It only became a complete whole when man was formed by God, since the manifestations

But you can reject both theses at once, and conceive the manifestations of evil in men as products of the activity of an acting Evil. Then you denounce evil as created, for the activity of an acting being has a beginning; and on the other hand you cannot make evil anything more than its manifestations in men, for what product of an evil activity do you have to point to outside of human actions? We have indeed seen that the acting being is not wickedness as a substance, but according to the way in which he conducts himself voluntarily, and in that he chooses to perform the acts which earn him the appellation of wicked: this is one of the premises that I have established in my demonstration. But you can reject both theses at once, and conceive the manifestations of evil in men as products of the activity of an acting Evil. Then you denounce evil as created, for the activity of an acting being has a beginning; and on the other hand you cannot make evil anything more than its manifestations in men, for what product of an evil activity do you have to point to outside of human actions? We have indeed seen that the acting being is not wickedness as a substance, but according to the way in which he conducts himself voluntarily, and in that he chooses to perform the acts which earn him the appellation of wicked: this is one of the premises that I have established in my demonstration.

Ταῆτι, πὸ οὰ [{--- Τὸν μὲν οὖν ἡμέτερον, ὦ φίλε, λόγον ἱκανῶς εἰρῇσθαί μοι δοκεῖ. Καὶ σὲ δὲ μὴ λανθάνειν νομίζω ὅτι ἕκαστος ἄνθρωπος τοὺς πλησίον '" παραιτεῖσθαι πέφυκεν εὐχολώτατος"
A. - The thesis that we had posited, my friend, has been discussed in a way that I consider satisfactory. But you yourself are not unaware, I think, that every man is by nature more than inclined to contest the allegations of others: it is only through a meticulous examination of things that one arrives at complete conviction. That is why, if you are willing, I beg you to give me in turn the role of investigator and to satisfy my desire to ask a few questions. It is the good way to surely reach the truth, without, moreover, my inquiry destroying your argumentation: let each of us engage in an equal search, it will be a guarantee for the knowledge of the truth. For it is certain that the truth often remains hidden
ἡ γὰρ τῶν πραγμάτων" ἀκριδὴς ἐξέτασις τὸν ἔλεγχον παρέχει σαφῶς. Ὅθεν, εἴ σοι φίλον ἐστί, καὶ ἐμοὶ παράσχες τὸν τῆς ζητήσεως λόγον βραχέα ἀναπυθέσθαι προαιρουμένῳ. Οὕτω γὰρ τὸ ἀληθὲς σαφῶς εὑρηθήσεσθαί μοι δοκεῖ, οὐχ ἀναιρουμένου τοῦ σοῦ λόγου τῇ ἐμῇ ἐρωτήσει: ἴσης γὰρ γενομένης τῆς ζητήσεως πρὸς ἑκάστου ἡμῶν, ὁποία ἐστὶν ἡ ἀλήθεια
for lack of sufficient examination. That is why I ask you to grant me this exchange of good offices: we will thus see which is the best thesis, which will obviously give each of us the possibility of adopting the right opinion with complete certainty

M. - But neither do I, my dear, intend to withdraw my reasoning from criticism, and I declare that I accept it even on the points that I have already established as premises, if they happen to constitute an obstacle to the discussion: it is with all my heart that I desire that my argumentation be submitted to examination, and I beg you to apply yourself to it with all your strength. I believe indeed that I will derive the greatest profit from it: a precise critique will earn me the ability to attach myself to the truth not by conjecture, but by an in-depth study. M. - Yes. A. - To be good is to do no evil, and to do it is to be evil? M. - But this evil to which your question refers, how do you understand it? Is it murder, adultery, and other similar crimes? Or is it all the punishments that divine justice inflicts on those who have had the temerity to commit these crimes, punishments which seem an evil to those who undergo them justly and who receive the punishment for the evil they have dared to accomplish? If you meant to speak of the evil which has already been the subject of our discussion, I deny that God is its author: it is for me absurd to attribute it to God, when one has its responsible authors before one's eyes.
εἶναι, δίκην λαμθάνουσιν διδοῦσιν ἀνθ᾽ ὧν ἐτόλμησαν κακῶν πράττειν; Εἰ μὲν οὖν περὶ τῶν πρότερον εἰρημένων" ἐποιήσω τὸν λόγον, τὸν θεὸν οὔ φημι τούτων ποιητήν" ἄλογον γὰρ οἶμαι εἶναι τὸ προσφέρεσθαι προσδάλλεσθαι τῷ θεῷ, ἐν τῷ φανερῷ ὄντων τῶν ταῦτα ἄὧμε λῃ πε ΔΈΛεΟΣ ΔΈΛΔΑ 3Ξλμχ ΩΤ ΒΟΓᾺ Α ὈΧΤΈΆΘΡΜΙΠΑ ΛΟ ΤΒόρΗΠΠΗ, Τὸ ἡ πὲ γε πὶ ὗλκὸ 31λ οοντα, Ἐκ πράβεληλ. But if it is the evil inflicted by God as punishment for evil actions that you mean to speak of, then I affirm that it is not an evil, but that it is equity. For it is obviously fitting that the author of evil receive the just punishment for his fault; and of justice, no one will say that it is an evil, except, however, the one who justly undergoes its effects. Thus currently, when our criminals are punished by the magistrates, I claim that the treatment they receive is a good. They do not say that it is a good, but an evil on the contrary: it is characteristic of evildoers to consider justice unjust. But we, we do not admit that it is an evil.

First, as for me, I affirm that nothing is evil by nature, and that a thing is only called evil from the point of view of the use one makes of it. <I will begin with the question of adultery>. The term adultery designates relations between a man and a woman. But if a man has relations with his wife with a view to procreation and to perpetuate his race, these relations are a good thing. If another abandons the commerce of his legitimate spouse to outrage the conjugal rights of another,

But I will say as much of murder. If someone wants to kill the adulterous man he has caught in the act, and demands justice for the crime that this man has had the temerity to accomplish, he does not act badly; if someone causes the death of one who is innocent of any crime, for no other motive than, for example, to take from him what he possesses, his money or his goods, he does evil. The act is the same in both cases, and it is the way of acting that produces the difference. Furthermore, taking money is also only an evil according to the way in which one takes it. Indeed, if a man shares his goods and gives a part of what he possesses, the one who takes the money does nothing wrong; if on the contrary he takes it from him against his will or secretly, he commits an evil action. In both cases, one takes the good of another, and it is the way of taking it that makes one say that it is evil.

But the art of making portraits or statues is not in itself an evil either: it can become so according to the way in which one uses it. Indeed, if a sculptor makes a statue that reproduces the human form, not as a work of art or because he desires to find in a human figuration the resembling image of a friend, but to render it worship and invoke it as a god, he commits an evil action. Thus it is the way of acting and it is the intention of the author that make him accomplish evil.

examples, I will move on to something else. Consider with me the case of iron: it too is sometimes a good and sometimes an evil, and it is the use one makes of it that shows this. M. - That men were created such by God, I do not admit at all. But I say that the first man was created master of his acts, that is to say free, and that the continuators of the species inherited from him the same freedom. Yes, I claim that man was born free, and I do not accept making him a slave. It is even, I say, the highest favor that God
ὀΑμοκλλος ΤΡΗΟΥ πὲ ΠρΆΒΟΜΟΥ ὙΛΚΟΥ ἘΜΤῊ ΥΛΑΤΟΛ, βέμ πε ΟΒΟΒΟΛΒΗδ, ὧτ πεέτοχε ὦ πρελμπιεὴ δ ρωλ’ τογαῖς πρηλπια ὁκοκολογ. βεκ πὸ ΟΕ ΟΕΟΒΟΛΗΟΥ ἘΜΤῊ κου, ΦΑΒΟΤΆΤΗ πὲ ἐμὸν ἰἄκοιε χομετα. ὧε ἘῸ ἐμ ὦ παιμεῖιεε λαφοβληϊε τ ΒΟΓᾺ φέκογ. ἸΆκοῦ φΑΞῈ ΟΔΟΒεΗ ΙΧ ἤηλ ΒΟΑ ΜΟΥΆΚΕ ΡΑΒΟΤΑ ΤΕ ἘΟΕῚ ΠΟΒΕΛΈΝΤΝ.
has done him, because indeed, outside of him, everything must servilely execute the orders of God. If you name the sky, it stands motionless, carrying its Master, without moving from the place that has been fixed for it. If it is a question of the sun, it accomplishes the movement that is prescribed for it without ever refusing to provide its course, like a slave forced to serve his master. As for the earth, you also see it, solidly established, which is subject to the instruction it has received. It is the same for the rest of the universe: everything serves its creator by necessity,

But man has obtained the faculty to obey whom he wishes and to choose his master himself, without being enslaved to a natural fatality or deprived of the power to act. And I say that this is a favor he has received for his profit, in order to allow him to obtain from his master some advantage in addition to those he possesses, an additional advantage that he draws from his obedience and that he claims as a due from his creator. For it is not, I affirm, a damage for man to have been thus created free, but a profit. Let us admit that God had made him like one of the elements of the universe or one of the beings that serve Him likewise by necessity: there is no longer for him any reward for his merit; he is only an instrument in the hands of the creator, and there is no reason for him to incur blame for what he has done of evil

since the responsibility for it falls on the one of whom he is the instrument; but there is also no knowledge for him of a superior good, and he knows nothing more than that for which he is destined by nature. αὐτῷ, κἂν τὴν ἐντολὴν λαμθάνῃ', τὴν δὲ τοῦ δύνασθαι" προαίρεσιν εἰς τὸ χρεῖττον τρέπειν ὁ θεὸς παραινεῖ. Ὥσπερ γὰρ πατὴρ παιδὶ παραινῶν", ἐξουσίαν ἔχοντι ἐκμανθάνειν τὰ μαθήματα, καὶ" μᾶλλον ἔχεσθαι τῶν μαθημάτων, ὅτι κρεῖττον τοῦτο" μηνύων, οὐ τὴν τοῦ δύνασθαι τοῦ παιδὸς ἐξουσίαν" ἀφαιρεῖ, χὰἂν μὴ ἑκὼν μανθάνειν βούληται, οὕτω μοι καὶ τὸν θεὸν οὐ δοκεῖ᾽ προτρέποντα τὸν ἄνθρωπον" πείθεσθαι τοῖς προστάγμασιν" ἀφαιρεῖν αὐτοῦ τὴν ἐξουσίαν ὁ τῆς προαιρέσεως, τῷ ' δύνασθαι χαὶ μὴ ὑπακούειν τοῖς προστάγμασιν' ἡ. Ὥστε οὐχ ἵνα τὴν ἐξουσίαν ἣν ἔδωχεν ἀφέλῃ προστάττειν βούλεται, ἀλλ᾽ ἵνα κρεῖττον δωρήσηται'" ὡς ἀξίῳ μειζόνων τυχεῖν, ἀνθ᾽ ὧν ὑπήκουσεν τῷ θεῷ χαὶ τὴν τοῦ μὴ ὑπακούειν ἐξουσίαν ἔχων. Φημὶ!" τοιγαροῦν τὸν θεόν, οὕτω τὸν ἄνθρωπον τιμῆσαι προαιρούμενον καὶ τῶν χρειτ- τόνων ἐπιστήμονα γενέσθαι '", τὴν ἐξουσίαν αὐτῷ τοῦ δύνασθαι ποιεῖν" ἃ βούλεται δεδωκέ- ναι' δ, καὶ τὴν ἐξουσίαν αὐτοῦ εἰς κρεῖττον «ἐπιστρέφειν; παραινεῖν 'ἧ, οὐκ ἀφαιρούμενον "᾿ πάλιν τὸ αὐτεξούσιον, ἀλλὰ τὸ κρεῖττον μηνῦσαι '" θέλοντα" τὸ μὲν γὰρ δύνασθαι πάρεστιν
in what the good consists. Man retains his power to act, even while receiving the instructions of God; God only advises him to orient the free use of this power toward the good. It is like when a father advises his child, who has the possibility of learning the sciences well, to apply himself even more to this study, by showing him its utility: the child is not deprived thereby of his freedom to act, even if he does not have the will to study of himself. I estimate likewise that God, by pressing man to obey His commandments, does not take away his free will, since man keeps the power not to obey His prescriptions. For, in itself, the fact that God thus gives him advice proves well that He has not taken away his freedom; and if God commands, it is to allow man to enjoy the superior good, a good that results from obedience to the order of God. Thus it is not to withdraw the freedom that He has granted that God intends to give orders, but to grant man an advantage, by making him worthy of obtaining a greater good in reward for the fact that he obeyed God when he had the faculty to disobey Him. Thus I claim that it is because God wanted, by this means, to honor man and give him the knowledge of the superior good that He granted him the power to do what he wanted; and He invites him to turn this faculty toward the good, not that He withdraws his freedom thereby, but because He wants to indicate to him

Ὥστε οὐχ ἵνα τὴν ἐξουσίαν ἣν ἔδωχεν ἀφέλῃ προστάττειν βούλεται, ἀλλ᾽ ἵνα κρεῖττον δωρήσηται'" ὡς ἀξίῳ μειζόνων τυχεῖν, ἀνθ᾽ ὧν ὑπήκουσεν τῷ θεῷ καὶ τὴν τοῦ μὴ ὑπακούειν ἐξουσίαν ἔχων.
in what the good consists. Man retains his power to act, even while receiving the instructions of God; God only advises him to orient the free use of this power toward the good. I estimate likewise that God, by pressing man to obey His commandments, does not take away his free will, since man keeps the power not to obey His prescriptions. For, in itself, the fact that God thus gives him advice proves well that He has not taken away his freedom; and if God commands, it is to allow man to enjoy the superior good, a good that results from obedience to the order of God. For God did not want to make such a gift, that of eternal incorruptibility, without reason: the gift would have been made without reason if it had been granted without justice, and it would not have been a just gift, if the one who received it had not had the double power: to obey the commandments of God, and also <not to obey them> if he did not want to. The just gift, indeed, is that
ἀλόγως δὲ γέγονε δ᾽ ἦν μὴ δικαίως διδομένη, οὐ γέγονε οὐκ ἦν δ᾽ ἀληθῶς ὀρθῶς δεδομένη "7, εἰ μιὴ ἀμφοτέρου εἶχεν ἐξουσίαν ὁ λαμιδάνων, πείθεσθαι '" ᾧ προστάττει ὁ θεός, ὡσαύτως καὶ «(μὴ ἐὰν μὴ θέλῃ'". Τὸ γὰρ δίκαιον δίδοται κατ᾽ ἀξίαν ὧν τις ἔπραξεν"
which is granted according to the merit of each person's actions. But how to make the difference between the actions of man, if he did not have the power to act in one way or the other <, to obey or to disobey>?

Οὐδὲ «(γὰρ;»" ἀγένητον ταύτην τις εἰπεῖν ἔχει, τοῦ ποιήσαντος αὐτὴν ὄντος γενητοῦ. Βολκο ἄξὲ ΒΞΗΜΒΕΙΙΗ Τὸ ὉΤΆΟΥΛΔ ὧὲ ΟΧΒΗΙ ἡ ΘΑ ΠρεολαιιλῊε. Βχοπμτληϊεῦ ΟὙΚΟΥΛΟΥ Ὑλκὸ 3λος ΟΔΟΥῚΗ οΑ.
I say that man was created provided with free will; not that I admit a pre-existing evil for which man would have received the power to opt if he wanted, but he received only the responsibility for obedience or disobedience to God: it is to that indeed that the granting of free will responded. Man, once created, receives an order from God, and from then on evil begins, man refusing to obey the divine prescription. And it was that alone which constituted evil, the disobedience to God, which had a beginning: for no one will go so far as to say that it was uncreated, since its author was created. I say that man was created provided with free will; not that I admit a pre-existing evil for which man would have received the power to opt if he wanted, but he received only the responsibility for obedience or disobedience to God: it is to that indeed that the granting of free will responded. Man, once created, receives an order from God, and from then on evil begins, man refusing to obey the divine prescription. And it was that alone which constituted evil, the disobedience to God, which had a beginning: for no one will go so far as to say that it was uncreated, since its author was created. But you are surely going to ask where this disobedience came from. The explanation is found in a certain divine book, and it is what authorizes me to say that man was not created by God provided with this inclination, but that he was only affected by it because someone taught it to him: man, indeed, had not been able to receive it as a trait of his nature, or else this

inclination would not have developed in him as a learned thing, since his nature itself would have included it. A certain divine word declares that man "learned evil"; and I say that what is taught to him is to disobey God, for that alone is the evil that happens against the desire of God. But you are surely going to ask where this disobedience came from. The explanation is found in a certain divine book, and it is what authorizes me to say that man was not created by God provided with this inclination, but that he was only affected by it because someone taught it to him: man, indeed, had not been able to receive it as a trait of his nature, or else this

Πβ΄. ἣς πὸ νό ἀπε ἀπὸ" κτὸ λπλ φλεὰ ἔληθογο ὑΛΕΟΤῈ Δὰ οχχρήνητε, ἀ Αρουσλτο Β ΟΙΗλ ΜΈΟΤΟ ΔᾺ ΟΧΤΈΘΡΗΤΕ,
Thus man does not learn to do evil by himself. Well, the one who teaches it to him is the Serpent, created by God Himself; and he teaches it to him with the intention of preventing him from attaining the superior good. If you still want to know the cause, it is that he bears envy toward man. And if you also ask the origin of this envy, it is because he did not receive the same honors as man. Indeed, man alone obtained being created "in the image and likeness" of God. But if you claim for this reason that God is responsible for evil, you are losing your common sense. For if God had taken from what the Serpent possessed to give it as a gift to man, He would have been truly the one responsible for evil; but if He maintained the Serpent as He had created him, and if He wanted to create man with these advantages, the responsibility goes to the Serpent and his envy. And indeed, if a master who has two slaves maintains one of them in servitude and adopts the other as a son, and if
ὠηχ χπὸ ΠορΕΒΝΟΒΛῈΧ ΔᾺ ΟΥ̓ΕΜδΤᾺ ΟΗηςΣ ΠΟΎΤΕΝΛΤΟ " ΒΛΛΑΜΚΟΥ, ΒΗΝΟΗΖΣ ΔῊ Ἀλλ- ἈΒΙΒΑ ΒΟΥΛΕΤΕ, ὠπόμπχ. 3λογῖ ΠΟΒΠΙΛΕΗΪΟ», ἩμΥτῦπεῦ πε τε ἢ ἐμὸν ἥπ μησρύτολιε ΟἹ ὀθόγο ΟΟΗΒ ΠΟΛΛΈΧ;
the first, seized with envy, kills the one whom his master has thus honored, will the master be responsible for this criminal enterprise, when he has taken nothing from this slave and has given nothing to the other of what belonged to him?

But you can still raise this problem: if there was no pre-existing evil, where did the knowledge of evil come from to the Serpent, whom I call the devil (the instigator of discord)? Here is the good answer to give to this objection: the devil perceived that evil consisted in disobedience
Ἀλλὰ μὴν καὶ περὶ τούτου ζητεῖν ἔχεις ζητήσεις), οὐδενὸς οὐδέ τινος ὄντος κακοῦ "΄, πόθεν καὶ τῷ ὄφει, ὃν διάδολον χαλῶ "ἡ, ἡ γνῶσις τοῦ κακοῦ. Ἀντιλέγεται δὲ τοῦτο χαλῶς ὅτι ὁ διάδολος τοῦτο κακὸν ἐνόησε τὸ παρακούειν θεοῦ, ὅθεν καὶ εἰς τοῦτο παρώρμησε τὸν
to God; and that is why he incited man to disobey. Let us imagine indeed an enemy who hides his hatred: he has the hidden intention of plunging into misfortune the one he hates, but he ignores the way to harm thanks to

«ἀκα. Πρ'. by which he might achieve his design. He learns that a man skilled in the art of medicine has prescribed to his neighbor—that is, to the one he wished to harm—the regimen he must follow to have health: whether he fixed for him the moment that alone is suitable for taking food, or whether he forbade him various foods, or else ordered him only to abstain from a specific food. And he, as soon as he is provided with this information, presents himself to this man while feigning friendship; he accuses the physician of having prescribed a harmful regimen, he advises him the regimen opposite to that of the physician, he convinces him, and thus he harms him. He did not know the way to harm before, but it is the physician's prescription that taught it to him. In the same way, consider with me that the devil first experienced envy, then he found himself ignorant of the manner in which he would do evil: there was indeed no pre-existing evil from which he could draw the knowledge that he

lacked. But he learned that God had given man an order that forbade him to take his food from a certain tree—without understanding, in my opinion, that the tree bore death or that it was not good for the food of man—either because the tree actually gave death and for this reason it had been forbidden to man to take of its fruits from which he
πὲ ἰἄκοκε ΑΡΈΒΟΥ Ομτα, ἄπογιμα πη ἰ ἡ ἄπο ὦ πὲ ἘΛΑΤΌΠΟΤΡΈΒΗΟΥ ὙΛΒΩΣΝ ΝᾺ ἄλε φαβαμέβα, ἤλη ἄμμο οΑλζ οαὗτη πολλίλιπε ὦ τοῦ ΛΈΛΑ ἘΧ3 ἘΦΆΝΗ οα ὙλκοΥ ΠΡΗΪΤῊ ΩΤ ΜΕΓῸ, ΤΥ πέγοσκε ὦ οὐ αδετη ππάῖπε ὙλΑϑ, ἦλη πλκε ἔσγοσκε πρεολογ- ἄνθρωπον. Ὥσπερ γὰρ ἐάν' τις ἐχθρὸς -«“ὧν"-᾿ ἀποκρύψηται τὴν ἔχθραν, λεληθότως «μὲν; βουλόμενος κακοῖς περιδαλεῖν, μὴ εἰδὼς ἀγνοῶν δὲ τὸν τρόπον τῆς κακίας καθ᾽ ὃν δυνήσεται ποιῆσαι τοῦθ᾽ ὅπερ ἐπινοεῖ, πυνθανόμενός" τινα τῆς ἰατρικῆς ἐπιστήμονα ὄντα ἐπαγγείλαντα τῷ πλησίον, τουτέστιν ἐχείνῳ ᾧ οὗτος ἤθελε ποιήσασθαι βλάδην, τοῦτον τὸν τρόπον καθ’ ὃν δυνήσεται ὑγιὴς εἶναι γενέσθαι ᾽, εἴτε χρόνον ἕνα ὡρίσατο ἐν ᾧ μόνον χρῆσθαι τῆς τροφῆς βρώσεως εἴτε τὴν τῶν πολλῶν ἐδεσμάτων ἀπόστασιν εἴτε τοῦτο ἔδεσμα
was to die, or else that disobedience to God had been for man a cause of death, for having done what God had disapproved. He persuaded man to transgress the order of God, without yet knowing in what the evil in this action resided. Then he perceived that God was blaming man for having disobeyed His order and that He was stripping him like a condemned man, and he understood then, not by the fact of his previous nature, but by the threats of God, in what the evil consisted. But you can still raise this problem: if there was no pre-existing evil, whence came to the Serpent, whom I call the devil (the instigator of discord), the knowledge of evil? Here is the correct answer to give to this objection: the devil perceived that evil consisted in disobedience


[p. 805] {#po.22.p805}
«“καὶ;:- μόνον φεύγοντα, ἐκεῖνος δ᾽ εὐθέως δὲ χαὶ ἅμα τε καὶ ἀχούσας ὡσπερεὶ μετὰ φιλίας ἐλθὼν χαὶ τὸν ἰατρὸν διαθαλὼν ὡς τὰ βλαδερὰ αὐτοῦ προστάξαντος αὐτῷ προστά- ξαντα)", προστάττῃ δὲ πάλιν τὰ ἐναντία ποιεῖν οἷς προσέταξεν ἐκεῖνος, καὶ οὕτω πείσας βλάθην ποιήσηται τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ, μὴ πρὸ τούτου προτοῦ εἰδὼς τὸν τρόπον τῆς κακίας, ἀλλ᾽ ἀπὸ τοῦ προστάγματος τοῦ ἰατροῦ μαθών’ ὁμοίως" μοι καὶ τὸν διάδολον ἐπινόησον πρῶτον" «“μὲν;» φθονῆσαι, εἶτα '5 μὴ εἰδέναι τὸν τρόπον πῶς ἂν ποιῆσαι ποιήσειε, πῶς ποιῆσαι δεῖ τὸ κακόν’ οὐ γὰρ ἦν πρότερον προῆν χαχὸν'' ἀφ᾽ οὗ ἂν ἐδυνήθη λαθεῖν τὴν γνῶσιν" εἰδὼς δὲ πρὸς τοῦ θεοῦ ἐντολὴν τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ δεδομένην ἐπέχουσαν τοῦ ἐδέσματος γεύσασθαι ἀπό τινος δένδρου, ὥς μοι δοχεῖ, οὐχ ὡς τοῦ δένδρου θάνατον ἔχοντος οὐδ᾽ ὡς "ἢ οὐχ εὔχρηστον τοῖς ἀνθρώποις πρὸς τὴν βρῶσιν νοήσας, εἴθ᾽ ὅτι τὸ φυτὸν τὸν θάνατον παρεῖχε καὶ τούτου ἕνεκα ἀπηγορεύθη τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ χαθδεῖν ἀπ’ αὐτοῦ, ἀφ᾽ οὗ καὶ ἀποθανεῖν ἹΠΑΤῊ ΕΟΓᾺ ΒΒΝΑ ἡ οαὐτα' ἍλΚΟΥ ἘΜ ἤπκοπε ὀχτβοριπαι οὗ ἐπε ὠταρίνλιε ΒΟΓΖ, πρῈΠΡῈ ΕΥΤΎᾺ Πρ τ ϑπητη ΞΑΠΟΒΈΛΕ πον, πὲ ἐψε ΒΈΛΜμΗ πος ἦχὰ ἔτι,
to God; and that is why he incited man to disobey. Let us imagine, indeed, an enemy who conceals his hatred: he has the hidden intention of plunging into misfortune the one he hates, but he is ignorant of the manner of harming by which he might achieve his design. He learns that a man skilled in the art of medicine has prescribed to his neighbor—that is, to the one he wished to harm—the regimen he must follow to have health: whether he fixed for him the moment that alone is suitable for taking food, or whether he forbade him various foods, or else ordered him only to abstain from a specific food. And he, as soon as he is provided with this information, presents himself to this man while feigning friendship; he accuses the physician of having prescribed a harmful regimen, he advises him the regimen opposite to that of the physician, he convinces him, and thus he harms him. He did not know the way to harm before, but it is the physician's prescription that taught it to him. In the same way, consider with me that the devil first experienced envy, then he found himself ignorant of the manner in which he would do evil: there was indeed no pre-existing evil from which he could draw the knowledge that he

ΠΛΆΜΚΖ πὲ ΠοΟΛΈπΕ ΕΟΓᾺ ΠΟΧΟΥΛΜΙΠΑ Ὑλπλ ὦ πηχηῖε πρέολογιια ΞΑΠΟΚΈΛΕ ἐτο ἡ κόρμοτε. ὠοογπεϊα ΒΞΕΠΈΔΙΟΙΒΑ, πε ΟὟ ἈΡΈΒΗΑΤΟ ἐοτμοταλ, ΗΖ ὧτ πρὲσ χμεηϊλ ποκα ΤΟΓΛᾺ φΑ 3ΞΟγ Ια ΚΑΚῸ ἐοτὰ 3λο.
